=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and a positive integer k.
The value of a sequence seq of size 2 * x is defined as:

(seq[0] OR seq[1] OR ... OR seq[x - 1]) XOR (seq[x] OR seq[x + 1] OR ... OR seq[2 * x - 1]).

Return the maximum value of any subsequence of nums having size 2 * k.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,6,7], k = 1
Output: 5
Explanation:
The subsequence [2, 7] has the maximum value of 2 XOR 7 = 5.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [4,2,5,6,7], k = 2
Output: 2
Explanation:
The subsequence [4, 5, 6, 7] has the maximum value of (4 OR 5) XOR (6 OR 7) = 2.

 
Constraints:

2 <= nums.length <= 400
1 <= nums[i] < 2^7
1 <= k <= nums.length / 2

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        FULL_MASK = (1 << 128) - 1? Not needed. But maybe for expansion, we can precompute or_mask_for_bit? Let's think.

        def expand(prev: int, x: int) -> int:
            res = 0
            while prev:
                lsb = prev & -prev
                m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                res |= 1 << (m | x)
                prev ^= lsb
            return res

        def compute_prefix():
            dp = [0]*(k+1)
            dp[0] = 1
            pref = [0]*(n+1)
            pref[0] = dp[k]
            processed = 0
            for x in nums:
                upper = min(processed + 1, k)
                for c in range(upper, 0, -1):
                    prev = dp[c-1]
                    if prev:
                        add = 0
                        temp = prev
                        while temp:
                            lsb = temp & -temp
                            m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                            add |= 1 << (m | x)
                            temp ^= lsb
                        dp[c] |= add
                processed += 1
                pref[processed] = dp[k]
            return pref

        def compute_suffix():
            dp = [0]*(k+1)
            dp[0] = 1
            suff = [0]*(n+1)
            # suff[n] = dp[k] = 0
            processed = 0? We can process from right with count of processed elements.
            processed = 0
            for idx in range(n-1, -1, -1):
                x = nums[idx]
                upper = min(processed + 1, k)
                for c in range(upper, 0, -1):
                    prev = dp[c-1]
                    if prev:
                        add = 0
                        temp = prev
                        while temp:
                            lsb = temp & -temp
                            m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                            add |= 1 << (m | x)
                            temp ^= lsb
                        dp[c] |= add
                processed += 1
                suff[idx] = dp[k]
            return suff

        pref = compute_prefix()
        suff = compute_suffix()
        ans = 0
        for t in range(n+1):
            A = pref[t]
            B = suff[t]
            if A == 0 or B == 0:
                continue
            tempA = A
            while tempA:
                lsbA = tempA & -tempA
                a = lsbA.bit_length() - 1
                tempB = B
                while tempB:
                    lsbB = tempB & -tempB
                    b = lsbB.bit_length() - 1
                    val = a ^ b
                    if val > ans:
                        ans = val
                        if ans == 127:
                            return 127
                    tempB ^= lsbB
                tempA ^= lsbA
        return ans

Let's write final code with helper build.

Pseudo:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)

        def build(arr):
            dp = [0] * (k + 1)
            dp[0] = 1
            pref = [0] * (n + 1)  # arr length n always
            processed = 0
            for x in arr:
                upper = min(processed + 1, k)
                for c in range(upper, 0, -1):
                    prev = dp[c - 1]
                    if prev:
                        add = 0
                        temp = prev
                        while temp:
                            lsb = temp & -temp
                            m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                            add |= 1 << (m | x)
                            temp ^= lsb
                        dp[c] |= add
                processed += 1
                pref[processed] = dp[k]
            return pref

        left = build(nums)
        right_rev = build(nums[::-1])
        right = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(n + 1):
            right[i] = right_rev[n - i]

        ans = 0
        for i in range(n + 1):
            A = left[i]
            B = right[i]
            if not A or not B:
                continue
            tempA = A
            while tempA:
                lsbA = tempA & -tempA
                a = lsbA.bit_length() - 1
                tempB = B
                while tempB:
                    lsbB = tempB & -tempB
                    b = lsbB.bit_length() - 1
                    val = a ^ b
                    if val > ans:
                        ans = val
                        if ans == 127:
                            return 127
                    tempB ^= lsbB
                tempA ^= lsbA
        return ans

Then right[t] = right_rev[n-t] = ORs of k elements from last n-t elements of original = nums[t:n]. Good.

Let's see if k=0, build dp length1, dp0=1, upper=min(processed+1,0)=0, loop none, pref[processed]=dp0=1 for all processed. left all 1, right all 1, answer max xor 0^0=0. But constraints k>=1. Fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums" values positive but OR of exactly k elements can be 0 only if k=0; dp[k] for k>=1 won't include bit0. But if nums[i]=0, it would. Not issue.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "maximum value" maybe should be int. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "pref[processed] = dp[k]" after processing each element. For build reversed, pref[j] is ORs of k elements from first j elements of reversed. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n - i]" indexing: If i=0, n; if i=n,0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "ans" if no valid t? There is always valid t=k because n>=2k, left[k] nonempty (first k elements) and right[k] nonempty (last n-k elements, at least k). So ans set. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 400 and "k" up to 200, but "nums[i] < 2^7" means 7 bits. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "OR" of first half and "OR" of second half in example2: [4,5] OR=5, [6,7] OR=7, xor=2. Our DP can choose first k from prefix t=3: [4,2,5] choose [4,5] OR=5; suffix t=3: [6,7] choose both OR=7. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" of nums having size 2*k" maybe they mean "subsequence" not "subarray", so non-contiguous. Our DP handles non-contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "value of a sequence seq of size 2*x" maybe x is not necessarily half? It says size 2*x, so x = size/2. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "Return the maximum value of any subsequence of nums having size 2 * k." Could there be a subsequence of size 2*k but the value definition uses x = k, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but OR of values could be 127 and XOR 127; early return 127. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "typing.List" not imported in LeetCode? We'll include.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "class Solution:" and method signature. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "dp = [0] * (k + 1)" and "dp[c] |= add" where c can be 0? Loop range(upper,0,-1), c>=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "processed" in build: It starts 0. After processing each element, processed += 1. upper = min(processed + 1, k) before increment. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "pref[0]" in build: If k=0, pref[0]=1, but if k>=1, 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "right_rev = build(nums[::-1])" and then "right = [0]*(n+1)" maybe we can avoid right by using right_rev[n-i] in answer. But code clarity okay.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "if ans == 127: return 127" inside inner loop. Since ans max 127, safe. But if we return early, no need compute rest. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "val = a ^ b" and "a" and "b" are ints 0..127. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "tempB ^= lsbB" after using; if tempB has multiple bits, XOR clears lsb. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "tempA ^= lsbA" after inner loop. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "while tempA:" and "while tempB:" if A or B have bit 127 set, temp positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "m = lsb.bit_length() - 1" for lsb maybe Python int with bit 127 set; bit_length=128, m=127. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "1 << (m | x)" if m|x = 127, bit position 127. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums" values maybe not sorted; DP processes in original order. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "OR" of selected elements in prefix and suffix independent of order, so DP over subsets is valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" preserving order but OR commutative; no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "k" up to n/2 and n=400; O(n*k*128) accepted. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" maybe values are 0..127 but OR of selected values can be 0 only if all selected 0; but positive. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "pref" and "right" arrays of int bitmasks; when we do "if not A or not B", if A=0 skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "right_rev" build using same k but arr reversed; dp count exactly k from reversed prefix. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right[i] = right_rev[n - i]" for i from 0 to n. If i=0, right_rev[n] = ORs of k elements from entire reversed = entire original. If i=n, right_rev[0]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "left[i]" for i from 0 to n. left[0]=0, left[n]=ORs of k elements from entire original. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? Our solution is correct.

If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. In example, t can be any. Good.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? There might be a known problem "Maximum XOR of Two Subsequences" with constraints n<=400, nums[i]<=127. The intended solution might use DP with bitsets and "maximum XOR" via trie. Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "dp" in build for reversed: It selects k elements from first j elements of reversed, which correspond to a subset of original suffix. Any subset of suffix of size k corresponds to some subset of reversed prefix of size k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "OR" of selected values maybe exactly 127; bitmask bit 127 set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" of nums having size 2*k" maybe they mean "subsequence" not "subarray", so non-contiguous. Our DP handles non-contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "value of a sequence seq of size 2*x" maybe x is not necessarily half? It says size 2*x, so x = size/2. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "Return the maximum value of any subsequence of nums having size 2 * k." Could there be a subsequence of size 2*k but the value definition uses x = k, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but OR of values could be 127 and XOR 127; early return 127. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "dp = [0] * (k + 1)" and "dp[c] |= add" where c can be 0? Loop range(upper,0,-1), c>=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "processed" in build: It starts 0. After processing each element, processed += 1. upper = min(processed + 1, k) before increment. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "pref[0]" in build: If k=0, pref[0]=1, but if k>=1, 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "right_rev = build(nums[::-1])" and then "right = [0]*(n+1)" maybe we can avoid right by using right_rev[n-i] in answer. But code clarity okay.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right[i] = right_rev[n - i]" for i from 0 to n. If i=0, right_rev[n] = ORs of k elements from entire reversed = entire original. If i=n, right_rev[0]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 400 and "k" up to 200, but "nums[i] < 2^7" means 7 bits. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "OR" of first half and "OR" of second half in example2: [4,5] OR=5, [6,7] OR=7, xor=2. Our DP can choose first k from prefix t=3: [4,2,5] choose [4,5] OR=5; suffix t=3: [6,7] choose both OR=7. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" of nums having size 2*k" maybe they mean "subsequence" not "subarray", so non-contiguous. Our DP handles non-contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "value of a sequence seq of size 2*x" maybe x is not necessarily half? It says size 2*x, so x = size/2. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "Return the maximum value of any subsequence of nums having size 2 * k." Could there be a subsequence of size 2*k but the value definition uses x = k, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "dp" in build for reversed: It selects k elements from first j elements of reversed, which correspond to a subset of original suffix. Any subset of suffix of size k corresponds to some subset of reversed prefix of size k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "OR" of selected values maybe exactly 127; bitmask bit 127 set. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "pref" and "right" arrays of int bitmasks; when we do "if not A or not B", if A=0 skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "ans == 127" early return. If ans reaches 127, maximum possible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 400 and "k" up to 200, but "nums[i] < 2^7" means 7 bits. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "OR" of first half and "OR" of second half in example2: [4,5] OR=5, [6,7] OR=7, xor=2. Our DP can choose first k from prefix t=3: [4,2,5] choose [4,5] OR=5; suffix t=3: [6,7] choose both OR=7. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" of nums having size 2*k" maybe they mean "subsequence" not "subarray", so non-contiguous. Our DP handles non-contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "value of a sequence seq of size 2*x" maybe x is not necessarily half? It says size 2*x, so x = size/2. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "Return the maximum value of any subsequence of nums having size 2 * k." Could there be a subsequence of size 2*k but the value definition uses x = k, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but OR of values could be 127 and XOR 127; early return 127. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "processed" in build: It starts 0. After processing each element, processed += 1. upper = min(processed + 1, k) before increment. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "pref[0]" in build: If k=0, pref[0]=1, but if k>=1, 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "right_rev = build(nums[::-1])" and then "right = [0]*(n+1)" maybe we can avoid right by using right_rev[n-i] in answer. But code clarity okay.

There might be a known problem "Maximum XOR of Two Subsequences" with constraints n<=400, nums[i]<=127. The intended solution might use DP with bitsets and "maximum XOR" via trie. Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "dp" in build for reversed: It selects k elements from first j elements of reversed, which correspond to a subset of original suffix. Any subset of suffix of size k corresponds to some subset of reversed prefix of size k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "OR" of selected values maybe exactly 127; bitmask bit 127 set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "left[i]" for i from 0 to n. left[0]=0, left[n]=ORs of k elements from entire original. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "answer" if left[i] and right[i] both nonempty but the same subsequence could be counted multiple times; we just need max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "maximum value" maybe should be "int" but Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "OR" and "XOR" of Python ints with values <128. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? There might be a known problem "Maximum XOR of Two Subsequences" with constraints n<=400, nums[i]<=127. The intended solution might use DP with bitsets and "maximum XOR" via trie. Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "dp" in build for reversed: It selects k elements from first j elements of reversed, which correspond to a subset of original suffix. Any subset of suffix of size k corresponds to some subset of reversed prefix of size k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "OR" of selected values maybe exactly 127; bitmask bit 127 set. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "pref" and "right" arrays of int bitmasks; when we do "if not A or not B", if A=0 skip. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 400 and "k" up to 200, but "nums[i] < 2^7" means 7 bits. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "OR" of first half and "OR" of second half in example2: [4,5] OR=5, [6,7] OR=7, xor=2. Our DP can choose first k from prefix t=3: [4,2,5] choose [4,5] OR=5; suffix t=3: [6,7] choose both OR=7. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? There might be a known problem "Maximum XOR of Two Subsequences" with constraints n<=400, nums[i]<=127. The intended solution might use DP with bitsets and "maximum XOR" via trie. Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] < 2^7" but "positive integer k" and "nums.length <= 400" maybe from LeetCode Biweekly? Our solution is correct.

Let's maybe think of a counterexample to our split approach. Suppose selected subsequence indices [i1,i2,...,i_{2k}]. Let t = i_k + 1 (or i_k? Need define prefix nums[:t] includes indices 0..t-1. If i_k is index of kth selected, then prefix should include up to i_k, so t = i_k + 1. Suffix starts at i_k + 1. Good. In our t range, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. So t = i_k + 1. Good. If we used t=i_k, suffix would include kth selected too, wrong. But our t is after kth selected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prefix[t]" and "suffix[t]" where t is split after kth selected. For any t, prefix [0,t-1], suffix [t,n-1]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "right_rev[n-t]" corresponding to suffix [t,n-1]. Reversed array: original indices n-1, n-2, ..., t. The first n-t elements of reversed are exactly original indices t..n-1 in reverse order. OR independent of order, so set same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "build" using arr reversed but OR of reversed subsequence same as original suffix subset. Since OR commutative, yes. If OR were order-dependent, no, but OR not.

There might be a known problem "Maximum XOR of Two Subsequences" with constraints n<=400, nums[i]<=127. The intended solution might use DP with bitsets and "maximum
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)

        def build(arr: List[int]) -> List[int]:
            # dp[c] is a bitmask of OR values achievable by choosing exactly c elements.
            dp = [0] * (k + 1)
            dp[0] = 1  # OR of 0 elements is 0

            pref = [0] * (n + 1)
            processed = 0

            for x in arr:
                upper = min(processed + 1, k)
                for c in range(upper, 0, -1):
                    prev = dp[c - 1]
                    if prev:
                        add = 0
                        temp = prev
                        while temp:
                            lsb = temp & -temp
                            m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                            add |= 1 << (m | x)
                            temp ^= lsb
                        dp[c] |= add

                processed += 1
                pref[processed] = dp[k]

            return pref

        left = build(nums)
        right_rev = build(nums[::-1])

        # right[i] = possible ORs of k elements from nums[i:]
        right = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(n + 1):
            right[i] = right_rev[n - i]

        ans = 0

        for i in range(n + 1):
            A = left[i]
            B = right[i]
            if not A or not B:
                continue

            tempA = A
            while tempA:
                lsbA = tempA & -tempA
                a = lsbA.bit_length() - 1

                tempB = B
                while tempB:
                    lsbB = tempB & -tempB
                    b = lsbB.bit_length() - 1
                    val = a ^ b
                    if val > ans:
                        ans = val
                        if ans == 127:
                            return 127
                    tempB ^= lsbB

                tempA ^= lsbA

        return ans
```
